Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding. Let me create a summary document with all the findings.

## Summary

I've conducted a thorough exploration of the terminal feature and web config system in the Moltis Rust codebase. Here are my findings:

### 1. **Terminal Feature - Server-Side Setup**

**File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/terminal.rs` (1000+ lines)

**Key Components:**
- **PTY Runtime**: Uses `portable_pty` crate for native PTY allocation and spawning shells
- **Tmux Integration**: Manages persistent terminal sessions via tmux (`moltis-host-terminal` session)
- **WebSocket Handler**: `api_terminal_ws_upgrade_handler()` at line 961
  - CSRF protection: Validates Origin header matches Host header (same-origin check)
  - Auth check: Validates session/API key via `websocket_header_authenticated()`
  - Loopback detection: `is_local_connection()` for local-only access control
  
**Key Functions:**
- `spawn_host_terminal_runtime()` - Creates PTY with configured size and persistence
- `handle_terminal_ws_connection()` - Main WebSocket connection loop
- `host_terminal_tmux_list_windows()` - Lists active tmux windows
- `host_terminal_tmux_create_window()` - Creates new tmux window

**Constants:**
- Session name: `moltis-host-terminal`
- Default size: 220 cols × 56 rows
- Max input: 8KB per message

### 2. **Route Registration**

**File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/lib.rs` (lines 115-122)

Routes are registered in `build_api_routes()`:
```rust
.route("/api/terminal/windows", get(...).post(...))
.route("/api/terminal/ws", get(api_terminal_ws_upgrade_handler))
```

**Endpoints:**
- `GET /api/terminal/windows` → `api_terminal_windows_handler()` - List tmux windows
- `POST /api/terminal/windows` → `api_terminal_windows_create_handler()` - Create new window
- `GET /api/terminal/ws` → WebSocket upgrade for PTY stream

### 3. **Client-Side Terminal JavaScript**

**File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/assets/js/page-terminal.js` (1000+ lines)

**Key Exports:**
- `initTerminal(container)` - Sets up xterm.js + WebSocket connection
- `teardownTerminal()` - Clean up on unmount

**Terminal Integration:**
- Uses **xterm.js** (`@xterm/xterm`) with FitAddon for responsive sizing
- WebSocket messages: `{ type: "input"|"resize"|"switch_window"|"control", ... }`
- Base64-encoded output from server
- Persistent localStorage key: `moltis.settings.terminal.tmuxInstallPromptSeen.v1`
- Auto-reconnect with exponential backoff (800ms delay)

**Window Management:**
- Fetches windows from `/api/terminal/windows`
- Supports switching between tmux windows via `?window=<id>` query param
- Renders tabs for each window with active state highlighting

### 4. **Config Schema & Web Section**

**File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/config/src/schema.rs` (lines 2124-2128)

```rust
pub struct WebConfig {
    pub search: WebSearchConfig,
    pub fetch: WebFetchConfig,
    pub firecrawl: FirecrawlConfig,
}
```

The `[web]` section in TOML contains web tools (search, fetch, Firecrawl) but does NOT yet contain terminal configuration. Terminal is currently hardcoded.

### 5. **Config Editor - Web Routes & API**

**File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/httpd/src/tools_routes.rs` (300+ lines)

**Endpoints:**
- `GET /api/config` → `config_get()` - Returns current TOML (preserves comments)
- `POST /api/config` → `config_save()` - Saves TOML to disk
- `POST /api/config/validate` - Validates TOML without saving
- `GET /api/config/template` - Returns documented template

**Security:**
- `require_config_access()` - Defense-in-depth check:
  - On localhost: allows if no password or auth disabled
  - Non-localhost: requires valid session/API key + middleware auth
- Uses `moltis_config::save_raw_config()` to preserve comments

### 6. **Client-Side Config Editor**

**File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/assets/js/page-settings.js` (lines 3047-3189)

**Key Functions:**
- `fetchConfig()` - Fetches current TOML from `/api/config`
- `onTest()` - Validates against `/api/config/validate`
- `onSave()` - POSTs to `/api/config`
- `onRestart()` - POSTs to `/api/restart`

**Workflow:**
1. User edits TOML in textarea
2. Click "Test" → POST to validate endpoint
3. Click "Save" → POST to save endpoint
4. Shows restart required message
5. Click "Restart" → POST to `/api/restart` with validation

### 7. **Gon Pattern (Server → Client Data Injection)**

**Server Side:**
- **File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/templates.rs` (lines 54-88)
- `GonData` struct contains: identity, version, port, counts, crons, sandbox, channels, etc.
- Built in `build_gon_data(gw: &GatewayState)` (lines 351+)
- Serialized as `window.__MOLTIS__` in HTML template

**Client Side:**
- **File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/assets/js/gon.js`
- `get(key)` - Retrieve value from `window.__MOLTIS__`
- `set(key, value)` - Update in-memory + notify listeners
- `onChange(key, fn)` - Register change callback
- `refresh()` - Re-fetch from `/api/gon` endpoint (cache-busting with timestamp)

**HTML Template:**
- **File:** `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/templates/index.html` (line 40)
- `<script nonce="{{ nonce }}">window.__MOLTIS__={{ gon_json|safe }};</script>`
- Injected into `<head>` before any module script runs

### 8. **Authentication & Auth Middleware**

**Auth Check on Terminal WebSocket:**
```rust
let header_authenticated = websocket_header_authenticated(
    &headers,
    state.gateway.credential_store.as_ref(),
    is_local
).await;
```

**Config Access Check:**
- Localhost (loopback TCP IP) without password = automatic allow
- Remote = requires auth via session/API key (validated by middleware)
- Uses `moltis_httpd::auth_middleware::check_auth()`

### 9. **Configuration Flow Summary**

```
Browser (page-settings.js)
  ↓ GET /api/config
Server (config_get)
  ↓ reads moltis.toml from disk
  ↓ returns raw TOML string (preserves comments)
Browser (textarea with syntax highlighting)
  ↓ User edits TOML
  ↓ POST /api/config/validate
Server validates via toml::from_str()
  ↓ returns warnings if any
Browser shows validation results
  ↓ User clicks Save
  ↓ POST /api/config with TOML
Server (config_save)
  ↓ saves via moltis_config::save_raw_config()
  ↓ returns restart_required: true
Browser shows "Restart required"
  ↓ User clicks Restart
  ↓ POST /api/restart
Server spawns new process, exits current one
```

### 10. **Key File Paths**

- **Terminal PTY**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/terminal.rs`
- **Terminal Client JS**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/assets/js/page-terminal.js`
- **Config Schema**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/config/src/schema.rs`
- **Config Routes**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/httpd/src/tools_routes.rs`
- **Config Editor Client**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/assets/js/page-settings.js`
- **Gon Pattern Server**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/templates.rs`
- **Gon Pattern Client**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/assets/js/gon.js`
- **Web Routes**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/lib.rs`
- **HTML Template**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/web/src/templates/index.html`

> AGENT

You raise a great point. If the config editor is accessible through the web UI, a `[web] terminal = false` flag alone is bypassable — someone who compromises auth (or has legitimate UI access but shouldn't have terminal) can just flip it back via the TOML editor and restart.

Here's what I'd propose:

**Two-layer approach:**

1. **Config field** `[web] terminal_enabled = true` (default) — for convenience, covers "I don't want terminal on this deployment." Can be toggled in the editor, requires restart.

2. **Environment variable** `MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED=1` — hard override that **wins** over config. Cannot be changed from the web UI. This is the real lockdown mechanism for `0.0.0.0` deployments.

**Enforcement points (server-side, all 3 terminal routes return 403 when disabled):**
- `GET /api/terminal/windows`
- `POST /api/terminal/windows`
- `GET /api/terminal/ws` (WebSocket upgrade)

**UI behavior when disabled:**
- Gon injects `terminal_enabled: false`
- Terminal tab still visible but shows a notice: "Terminal has been disabled by the server administrator"
- No WebSocket connection attempted
- If someone crafts a direct WS connection, the server rejects it (defense in depth)

**Why keep the tab visible?** Hiding it silently is confusing — users wonder if it's a bug. Showing a clear message makes the admin's intent visible.

**Why env var wins?** Same pattern as `MOLTIS_VERSION` — env vars represent deployment-level decisions that are outside the app's self-modification scope. The config editor can't write env vars, so this is a genuine hard boundary.

The config editor "bypass" concern is somewhat mitigated by the fact that anyone with config editor access already has significant control (they can change API keys, providers, restart the server). But for terminal specifically — because it's an unsandboxed shell — the env var override gives admins a way to say "no terminal, period, regardless of what the TOML says."

Should I enter plan mode to work through the implementation details, or does this direction need adjustments first?

   946→            )
   947→                .into_response(),
   948→        },
   949→        Err(err) => (
   950→            StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR,
   951→            Json(terminal_error(
   952→                TERMINAL_WINDOW_CREATE_FAILED,
   953→                err.to_string(),
   954→            )),
   955→        )
   956→            .into_response(),
   957→    }
   958→}
   959→
   960→/// Dedicated host terminal WebSocket stream (`Settings > Terminal`).
   961→pub async fn api_terminal_ws_upgrade_handler(
   962→    ws: WebSocketUpgrade,
   963→    Query(query): Query<HostTerminalWsQuery>,
   964→    headers: axum::http::HeaderMap,
   965→    ConnectInfo(addr): ConnectInfo<SocketAddr>,
   966→    State(state): State<AppState>,
   967→) -> impl IntoResponse {
   968→    // CSWSH protection: only same-origin browser upgrades are allowed.
   969→    if let Some(origin) = headers
   970→        .get(axum::http::header::ORIGIN)
   971→        .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok())
   972→    {
   973→        let host = headers
   974→            .get(axum::http::header::HOST)
   975→            .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok())
   976→            .unwrap_or("");
   977→        if !is_same_origin(origin, host) {
   978→            warn!(
   979→                origin,
   980→                host,
   981→                remote = %addr,
   982→                "rejected cross-origin terminal WebSocket upgrade"
   983→            );
   984→            return (
   985→                StatusCode::FORBIDDEN,
   986→                "cross-origin WebSocket connections are not allowed",
   987→            )
   988→                .into_response();
   989→        }
   990→    }
   991→
   992→    let is_local = is_local_connection(&headers, addr, state.gateway.behind_proxy);
   993→    let header_authenticated =
   994→        websocket_header_authenticated(&headers, state.gateway.credential_store.as_ref(), is_local)
   995→            .await;
   996→    if !header_authenticated {
   997→        return (
   998→            StatusCode::UNAUTHORIZED,
   999→            Json(terminal_error(
  1000→                "AUTH_NOT_AUTHENTICATED",
  1001→                "not authenticated",
  1002→            )),
  1003→        )
  1004→            .into_response();
  1005→    }
  1006→
  1007→    let requested_window = query.window;
  1008→    ws.on_upgrade(move |socket| handle_terminal_ws_connection(socket, addr, requested_window))
  1009→        .into_response()
  1010→}
  1011→
  1012→// ── WebSocket helpers ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1013→
  1014→async fn terminal_ws_send_json(
  1015→    ws_tx: &mut futures::stream::SplitSink<WebSocket, Message>,
  1016→    payload: serde_json::Value,
  1017→) -> bool {
  1018→    match serde_json::to_string(&payload) {
  1019→        Ok(text) => ws_tx.send(Message::Text(text.into())).await.is_ok(),
```

    12→/// Severity level for a diagnostic.

    14→pub enum Severity {
    15→    Error,
    16→    Warning,
    17→    Info,
    18→}
    19→
    20→impl std::fmt::Display for Severity {
    21→    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
    22→        match self {
    23→            Self::Error => write!(f, "error"),
    24→            Self::Warning => write!(f, "warning"),
    25→            Self::Info => write!(f, "info"),
    26→        }
    27→    }
    28→}
    29→
    30→/// A single validation diagnostic.
    31→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    32→pub struct Diagnostic {
    33→    pub severity: Severity,
    34→    /// Category: "syntax", "unknown-field", "deprecated-field", "unknown-provider", "type-error",
    35→    /// "security", "file-ref"
    36→    pub category: &'static str,
    37→    /// Dotted path, e.g. "server.bnd"
    38→    pub path: String,
    39→    pub message: String,
    40→}
    41→
    42→/// Result of validating a configuration file.

    44→pub struct ValidationResult {
    45→    pub diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic>,

   811→/// Gateway server configuration.
   812→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
   813→#[serde(default)]
   814→pub struct ServerConfig {
   815→    /// Address to bind to. Defaults to "127.0.0.1".
   816→    pub bind: String,
   817→    /// Port to listen on. When a new config is created, a random available port
   818→    /// is generated so each installation gets a unique port.
   819→    pub port: u16,
   820→    /// Enable verbose Axum/Tower HTTP request logs (`http_request` spans).
   821→    /// Useful for debugging redirects and request flow.
   822→    pub http_request_logs: bool,
   823→    /// Enable WebSocket request/response logs (`ws:` entries).
   824→    /// Useful for debugging RPC calls from the web UI.
   825→    pub ws_request_logs: bool,
   826→    /// Maximum number of log entries kept in the in-memory ring buffer.
   827→    /// Older entries are persisted to disk and available via the web UI.
   828→    /// Defaults to 1000. Increase for busy servers, decrease for memory-constrained devices.
   829→    #[serde(default = "default_log_buffer_size")]
   830→    pub log_buffer_size: usize,
   831→    /// URL of the releases manifest (`releases.json`) used by the update checker.
   832→    ///
   833→    /// Defaults to `https://www.moltis.org/releases.json` when unset.
   834→    pub update_releases_url: Option<String>,
   835→    /// Maximum number of SQLite pool connections. Lower values reduce memory
   836→    /// usage for personal gateways. Defaults to 5.
   837→    #[serde(default = "default_db_pool_max_connections")]
   838→    pub db_pool_max_connections: u32,
   839→    /// Base URL for the Shiki syntax-highlighting library loaded by the web UI.
   840→    ///
   841→    /// Defaults to `https://esm.sh/shiki@3.2.1?bundle` when unset.
   842→    /// Set to an alternative CDN or a self-hosted URL to override.
   843→    pub shiki_cdn_url: Option<String>,
   844→}
   845→
   846→fn default_log_buffer_size() -> usize {
   847→    1000
   848→}
   849→
   850→fn default_db_pool_max_connections() -> u32 {
   851→    5
   852→}
   853→
   854→impl Default for ServerConfig {
   855→    fn default() -> Self {

   857→            bind: "127.0.0.1".into(),

    60→
    61→function readTmuxInstallPromptSeen() {
    62→	try {
    63→		if (typeof localStorage === "undefined") return false;
    64→		return localStorage.getItem(TmuxInstallPromptStorageKey) === "1";
    65→	} catch {
    66→		return false;
    67→	}
    68→}
    69→
    70→function markTmuxInstallPromptSeen() {
    71→	tmuxInstallPromptSeen = true;
    72→	try {
    73→		if (typeof localStorage !== "undefined") {
    74→			localStorage.setItem(TmuxInstallPromptStorageKey, "1");
    75→		}
    76→	} catch {
    77→		// Ignore storage write errors in private/incognito contexts.
    78→	}
    79→}
    80→
    81→function clearObservers() {
    82→	if (resizeObserver) {
    83→		resizeObserver.disconnect();
    84→		resizeObserver = null;
    85→	}
    86→	if (themeObserver) {
    87→		themeObserver.disconnect();
    88→		themeObserver = null;
    89→	}
    90→	if (windowResizeListener) {
    91→		window.removeEventListener("resize", windowResizeListener);
    92→		windowResizeListener = null;
    93→	}
    94→	if (fontsReadyListener && typeof document !== "undefined" && document.fonts?.removeEventListener) {
    95→		document.fonts.removeEventListener("loadingdone", fontsReadyListener);
    96→		fontsReadyListener = null;
    97→	}
    98→}
    99→
   100→function clearScheduledFit() {
   101→	if (fitRaf) {
   102→		cancelAnimationFrame(fitRaf);

   104→	}
   105→}
   106→
   107→function clearReconnectTimer() {
   108→	if (reconnectTimer) {
   109→		clearTimeout(reconnectTimer);
   110→		reconnectTimer = null;
   111→	}
   112→}
   113→
   114→function clearResizeSettleTimers() {
   115→	for (const timer of resizeSettleTimers) {
   116→		clearTimeout(timer);
   117→	}
   118→	resizeSettleTimers = [];
   119→}
   120→
   121→function clearInputQueue() {
   122→	if (inputFlushTimer) {
   123→		clearTimeout(inputFlushTimer);
   124→		inputFlushTimer = null;
   125→	}
   126→	pendingInput = "";
   127→}

   351→/// Immutable fields and atomics live directly on this struct (no lock needed).

   983→
   984→/// Dedicated host terminal WebSocket stream (`Settings > Terminal`).
   985→pub async fn api_terminal_ws_upgrade_handler(
   986→    ws: WebSocketUpgrade,
   987→    Query(query): Query<HostTerminalWsQuery>,
   988→    headers: axum::http::HeaderMap,
   989→    ConnectInfo(addr): ConnectInfo<SocketAddr>,
   990→    State(state): State<AppState>,
   991→) -> impl IntoResponse {
   992→    // CSWSH protection: only same-origin browser upgrades are allowed.
   993→    if let Some(origin) = headers
   994→        .get(axum::http::header::ORIGIN)
   995→        .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok())
   996→    {
   997→        let host = headers
   998→            .get(axum::http::header::HOST)
   999→            .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok())
  1000→            .unwrap_or("");
  1001→        if !is_same_origin(origin, host) {
  1002→            warn!(

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/config/src/schema.rs",
  "old_string": "        let serialized = toml::to_string(&entry).unwrap();\n        assert!(\n            serialized.contains(\"wire_api = \\\"responses\\\"\"),\n            \"non-default wire_api should be serialized\"\n        );\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "        let serialized = toml::to_string(&entry).unwrap();\n        assert!(\n            serialized.contains(\"wire_api = \\\"responses\\\"\"),\n            \"non-default wire_api should be serialized\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn terminal_enabled_defaults_to_true() {\n        let cfg: MoltisConfig = toml::from_str(\"\").unwrap();\n        assert!(cfg.server.terminal_enabled);\n        assert!(cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn terminal_enabled_parsed_from_config() {\n        let cfg: MoltisConfig =\n            toml::from_str(\"[server]\\nterminal_enabled = false\\n\").unwrap();\n        assert!(!cfg.server.terminal_enabled);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn terminal_disabled_env_overrides_config() {\n        let cfg: MoltisConfig = toml::from_str(\"\").unwrap();\n        assert!(cfg.server.terminal_enabled);\n\n        // env var override\n        std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"1\");\n        assert!(!cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n        std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n\n        // true (case-insensitive)\n        std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"True\");\n        assert!(!cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n        std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n\n        // non-matching value does not override\n        std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"0\");\n        assert!(cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n        std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n    }\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn terminal_disabled_env_overrides_config() {\n        let cfg: MoltisConfig = toml::from_str(\"\").unwrap();\n        assert!(cfg.server.terminal_enabled);\n\n        // env var override\n        std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"1\");\n        assert!(!cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n        std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n\n        // true (case-insensitive)\n        std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"True\");\n        assert!(!cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n        std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n\n        // non-matching value does not override\n        std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"0\");\n        assert!(cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n        std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n    }",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn terminal_disabled_env_overrides_config() {\n        let cfg: MoltisConfig = toml::from_str(\"\").unwrap();\n        assert!(cfg.server.terminal_enabled);\n\n        // SAFETY: test runs are single-threaded for this env var; no other\n        // thread reads MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED concurrently.\n        unsafe {\n            // env var override\n            std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"1\");\n            assert!(!cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n            std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n\n            // true (case-insensitive)\n            std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"True\");\n            assert!(!cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n            std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n\n            // non-matching value does not override\n            std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"0\");\n            assert!(cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n            std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n        }\n    }"
}

No other tests use `set_var`. The lint forbids unsafe code entirely. I'll remove the env var test and instead test the logic indirectly — the `is_terminal_enabled` method can be tested for the config-only path, and the env var path is a simple `std::env::var` read that's straightforward enough.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/config/src/schema.rs",
  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn terminal_disabled_env_overrides_config() {\n        let cfg: MoltisConfig = toml::from_str(\"\").unwrap();\n        assert!(cfg.server.terminal_enabled);\n\n        // SAFETY: test runs are single-threaded for this env var; no other\n        // thread reads MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED concurrently.\n        unsafe {\n            // env var override\n            std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"1\");\n            assert!(!cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n            std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n\n            // true (case-insensitive)\n            std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"True\");\n            assert!(!cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n            std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n\n            // non-matching value does not override\n            std::env::set_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\", \"0\");\n            assert!(cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n            std::env::remove_var(\"MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED\");\n        }\n    }",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn terminal_disabled_via_config_reflects_in_helper() {\n        let cfg: MoltisConfig =\n            toml::from_str(\"[server]\\nterminal_enabled = false\\n\").unwrap();\n        // When the env var is not set, the helper returns the config value.\n        // (We cannot test the env-var override here because workspace lints\n        // deny unsafe code, and `std::env::set_var` is unsafe.)\n        assert!(!cfg.server.is_terminal_enabled());\n    }"
}

+impl ServerConfig {
+    /// Returns whether the web UI terminal is enabled, accounting for the
+    /// `MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED` env-var override. When the env var is set
+    /// to `"1"` or `"true"` (case-insensitive), the terminal is disabled
+    /// regardless of the config file value.
+    pub fn is_terminal_enabled(&self) -> bool {
+        if let Ok(val) = std::env::var("MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED")
+            && (val.eq_ignore_ascii_case("true") || val == "1")
+        {
+            return false;
         }
+        self.terminal_enabled

> AGENT

Done. Here's a summary of what was implemented across 6 files:

**Config layer** (`crates/config/src/schema.rs`):
- Added `terminal_enabled: bool` to `ServerConfig` (defaults to `true`)
- Added `ServerConfig::is_terminal_enabled()` — checks `MOLTIS_TERMINAL_DISABLED` env var first (wins if `"1"` or `"true"`), then falls back to the config value
- 3 tests covering defaults, parsing, and the helper method

**Validation** (`crates/config/src/validate.rs`):
- Added `terminal_enabled` to `build_schema_map()` so it's recognized as a known field
- Test confirming no unknown-field warning

**Config template** (`crates/config/src/template.rs`):
- Added `terminal_enabled = true` with comments explaining the env var override, including the note you requested about the web UI config editor limitation

**Server enforcement** (`crates/web/src/terminal.rs`):
- All 3 terminal routes (`GET /api/terminal/windows`, `POST /api/terminal/windows`, `GET /api/terminal/ws`) return 403 when disabled — defense in depth regardless of UI

**Frontend** (`crates/web/src/templates.rs` + `page-settings.js`):
- `terminal_enabled` injected via gon pattern
- Terminal tab stays visible but shows "The host terminal has been disabled by the server administrator" with re-enable instructions when disabled

Excellent! I now have a comprehensive understanding of the brute-force protections. Let me create a detailed summary:

## Comprehensive Brute-Force Protection Analysis

Based on my thorough exploration of the Moltis authentication system, here's what I found:

### **1. PASSWORD AUTHENTICATION FLOW**

**Location**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/httpd/src/auth_routes.rs` (line 286-325)

**Handler**: `login_handler()`

**Password Verification**: Uses `CredentialStore::verify_password()` from `crates/auth/src/credential_store.rs` (line 459-468)
- Uses **Argon2 password hashing** with proper salt generation
- Returns early on empty password hash (defense against timing attacks)
- However, **NO per-user failed attempt tracking** — the handler simply returns 401 on incorrect password

### **2. RATE LIMITING / REQUEST THROTTLING** ✓ PRESENT

**Location**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/httpd/src/request_throttle.rs` (comprehensive implementation)

**Key Details**:
- **IP-based rate limiting** using a `DashMap<ThrottleKey, WindowState>` for thread-safe, lock-free counting
- **Per-endpoint rate limits** classified by scope (`ThrottleScope` enum):
  - **`Login` scope** (`/api/auth/login` POST): **5 requests per 60 seconds** (line 93-96)
  - **`AuthApi` scope** (all `/api/auth/*`): **120 requests per 60 seconds** (line 98-101)
  - **`Api` scope** (all `/api/*`): **180 requests per 60 seconds** (line 103-106)
  - **`Share` scope** (`/share/*`): **90 requests per 60 seconds** (line 108-111)
  - **`Ws` scope** (`/ws/*`): **30 requests per 60 seconds** (line 113-116)

- **Response**: HTTP 429 (Too Many Requests) with `Retry-After` header (line 259-285)
- **Window tracking**: Sliding window with automatic cleanup every 512 requests (lines 194-200)
- **Cleanup mechanism**: Stale buckets removed after 3× max window duration

**Middleware Stack Order** (`server.rs` line 653-656):
```
throttle_gate → auth_gate → vault_guard → apply_middleware_stack
```
The throttle is applied **after authentication checks**, meaning rate limiting applies even before auth validation.

### **3. PASSKEY/WEBAUTHN AUTHENTICATION FLOW**

**Location**: `/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/httpd/src/auth_routes.rs` (line 803-830)

**Handler**: `passkey_auth_finish_handler()`

**WebAuthn State**:
- Challenges stored in `DashMap<String, PendingAuthentication>` with **5-minute TTL** (line 177-181, webauthn.rs)
- Challenge ID lookup and validation (line 192-195, webauthn.rs)
- Expired challenges are cleaned up automatically (line 224-230, webauthn.rs)

**Protection**: 
- Challenge must be valid and non-expired (lines 195-199, webauthn.rs)
- ALL `/api/auth/*` endpoints (including passkey endpoints) are covered by the **120 req/60sec rate limit** (request_throttle.rs line 46-47)
- **NO per-user failed passkey attempt tracking** — just the IP-based throttle

### **4. REQUEST THROTTLE MIDDLEWARE** ✓ IMPLEMENTED

**Location**: `crates/httpd/src/request_throttle.rs` (complete implementation)

**Scope Classification** (lines 42-59):
- Identifies endpoint type from HTTP method + path
- `/api/auth/login` → `Login` (strictest: 5/60s)
- `/api/auth/*` → `AuthApi` (120/60s)
- `/api/*` → `Api` (180/60s)
- Unauthenticated requests also throttled

**Bypass Logic** (lines 246-257):
- **Authenticated sessions bypass throttling** — only unauthenticated IPs are throttled
- No allowlisting/IP-whitelisting for auth endpoints specifically

**Client IP Resolution** (lines 287-334):
- Respects `X-Forwarded-For` (first value), `X-Real-IP`, `CF-Connecting-IP` headers
- Configurable proxy support via `state.gateway.behind_proxy`

### **5. AUTH MIDDLEWARE** ✓ PRESENT

**Location**: `crates/httpd/src/auth_middleware.rs`

**Key Function**: `check_auth()` (lines 50-94)
- Single source of truth for auth decisions
- Checks session cookie, API key, or loopback bypass
- Returns `AuthResult::Allowed`, `SetupRequired`, or `Unauthorized`

**Protection Scope**:
- Covers entire router via `auth_gate` middleware
- Public paths exempted: `/api/auth/*`, `/api/public/*`, `/assets/*`, etc.

### **6. CREDENTIAL STORE** 

**Location**: `crates/auth/src/credential_store.rs`

**Database Schema** (lines 248-293, migrations):
- `auth_password` table: single row with password hash (no attempt tracking)
- `passkeys` table: registered passkeys only
- `auth_sessions` table: session tokens with 30-day expiry
- **NO `failed_attempts` column or audit table** for login attempts

**Methods**:
- `verify_password()` (line 459): Single check, no counter
- `change_password()` (line 474): Invalidates all sessions (defense-in-depth)

### **7. PASSWORD VERIFICATION SECURITY**

**Timing Attack Protection**: ✓ Partially
- Argon2 inherently uses constant-time comparison
- However, early return on missing hash (line 465) creates minor timing leak (expected behavior)

**Hashing Algorithm**: ✓ Strong
- Argon2 with `OsRng` for salt generation
- No magic constants or insecure fallbacks

### **8. TESTS CONFIRMING PROTECTIONS**

**Location**: `crates/httpd/tests/auth_middleware.rs`

**Test 1** (line 1221): `login_endpoint_rate_limited_after_repeated_failures()`
- Confirms 5 failed attempts allowed
- 6th attempt returns HTTP 429
- Validates `Retry-After` header presence

**Test 2** (line 1270): `api_endpoint_rate_limited_after_high_request_volume()`
- Confirms 180 API requests allowed within window
- 181st request returns HTTP 429

### **CRITICAL GAPS FOUND**

1. **No per-user login attempt counter** — Only IP-based throttling
   - Attacker with rotating IPs can make unlimited attempts
   - No account lockout after N failed attempts
   
2. **No exponential backoff** — Fixed window with hard cutoff
   - After throttle expires (60 seconds), full rate restored immediately
   
3. **No password attempt logging/audit trail** — No database record of failed attempts
   - No way to detect distributed brute-force attacks post-fact
   - No alert mechanism on suspicious patterns
   
4. **Throttle bypassed by authenticated users** — Rate limiting disabled once logged in
   - Not a security gap but worth noting for DoS considerations
   
5. **Challenge reuse protection only by TTL** — No attempt counter on failed passkey verifications
   - Passkey challenges can be replayed until 5-minute expiry
   - No per-credential attempt tracking

6. **No IP-based blocking/banning** — After rate limit expires, attacker can resume
   - No temporary IP block even after repeated violations
   - No persistent ban list

### **RECOMMENDATIONS**

1. Add a `auth_login_attempts` table to track failed attempts per IP + optional username
2. Implement account lockout (e.g., 10 failed attempts = 30-minute lock)
3. Add exponential backoff instead of hard window reset
4. Log all failed authentication attempts for audit/alerting
5. Consider IP reputation/blocking after repeated violations
6. Add CAPTCHA after N failed attempts
7. Implement rate limiting on setup/initial password endpoints

---

**Summary**: The system has **IP-based request throttling** on all auth endpoints with strict limits on `/api/auth/login` (5 req/60s), but **lacks per-user login attempt tracking**, account lockout, and logging of failed attempts. This provides basic protection against simple brute-force attacks but is insufficient for sophisticated scenarios with IP rotation or distributed attacks.

> AGENT

Here's the current state:

**What exists (throttling):**
- IP-based rate limiting on `/api/auth/login`: **5 requests per 60 seconds** — returns HTTP 429 with `Retry-After` header
- All `/api/auth/*` endpoints (including passkey): **120 requests per 60 seconds**
- Tests confirm this works (`login_endpoint_rate_limited_after_repeated_failures` in `crates/httpd/tests/auth_middleware.rs`)

**What's missing (lockout/blocking):**
- **No per-user failed attempt counter** — only IP-based. An attacker rotating IPs bypasses the throttle entirely
- **No account lockout** after N failures — once the 60-second window expires, the full 5-attempt budget resets immediately
- **No exponential backoff** — fixed window, no progressive penalty
- **No audit log** of failed login attempts — no way to detect distributed brute-force after the fact
- **No IP banning** after repeated rate-limit violations

So the answer is: **(1) throttled — yes**, at 5 attempts/minute per IP. **(2) blocked after repeated failures — no**, there's no lockout mechanism.

The 5/60s throttle is reasonable protection against naive brute-force, but for a `0.0.0.0` deployment the gaps are real. Want me to file an issue for the missing lockout/audit trail, or work on it now?

   462→                .fetch_optional(&self.pool)
   463→                .await?;

   470→    /// Change the password (requires correct current password).
   471→    ///
   472→    /// Invalidates all existing sessions for defense-in-depth — even if the
   473→    /// WebSocket disconnect misses a client, old sessions cannot be reused.
   474→    pub async fn change_password(&self, current: &str, new_password: &str) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
   475→        if !self.verify_password(current).await? {
   476→            anyhow::bail!("current password is incorrect");
   477→        }
   478→        let hash = hash_password(new_password)?;
   479→        sqlx::query(
   480→            "UPDATE auth_password SET password_hash = ?, updated_at = datetime('now') WHERE id = 1",
   481→        )
   482→        .bind(&hash)
   483→        .execute(&self.pool)
   484→        .await?;
   485→
   486→        // Invalidate all sessions so old cookies cannot be reused.
   487→        sqlx::query("DELETE FROM auth_sessions")
   488→            .execute(&self.pool)

   120→
   121→enum ThrottleDecision {
   122→    Allowed,
   123→    Denied { retry_after: Duration },
   124→}
   125→
   126→impl RequestThrottle {
   127→    #[must_use]
   128→    pub fn new() -> Self {
   129→        Self::with_limits(ThrottleLimits::default())
   130→    }
   131→
   132→    fn with_limits(limits: ThrottleLimits) -> Self {
   133→        Self {
   134→            limits,
   135→            buckets: Arc::new(DashMap::new()),
   136→            requests_seen: Arc::new(AtomicU64::new(0)),
   137→        }
   138→    }
   139→
   140→    fn limit_for(&self, scope: ThrottleScope) -> RateLimit {
   141→        match scope {
   142→            ThrottleScope::Login => self.limits.login,
   143→            ThrottleScope::AuthApi => self.limits.auth_api,
   144→            ThrottleScope::Api => self.limits.api,
   145→            ThrottleScope::Share => self.limits.share,
   146→            ThrottleScope::Ws => self.limits.ws,
   147→        }
   148→    }
   149→
   150→    fn check(&self, ip: IpAddr, scope: ThrottleScope) -> ThrottleDecision {
   151→        self.check_at(ip, scope, Instant::now())
   152→    }
   153→
   154→    fn check_at(&self, ip: IpAddr, scope: ThrottleScope, now: Instant) -> ThrottleDecision {
   155→        let limit = self.limit_for(scope);
   156→        if limit.max_requests == 0 {
   157→            return ThrottleDecision::Denied {
   158→                retry_after: limit.window.max(Duration::from_secs(1)),
   159→            };
   160→        }
   161→
   162→        let key = ThrottleKey { ip, scope };
   163→        let decision = match self.buckets.entry(key) {
   164→            Entry::Occupied(mut occupied) => {
   165→                let state = occupied.get_mut();
   166→                let elapsed = now.duration_since(state.started_at);
   167→                if elapsed >= limit.window {
   168→                    state.started_at = now;
   169→                    state.count = 1;
   170→                    ThrottleDecision::Allowed
   171→                } else if state.count < limit.max_requests {
   172→                    state.count += 1;
   173→                    ThrottleDecision::Allowed
   174→                } else {
   175→                    ThrottleDecision::Denied {
   176→                        retry_after: limit.window.saturating_sub(elapsed),
   177→                    }
   178→                }
   179→            },
   180→            Entry::Vacant(vacant) => {
   181→                vacant.insert(WindowState {
   182→                    started_at: now,
   183→                    count: 1,
   184→                });
   185→                ThrottleDecision::Allowed
   186→            },
   187→        };
   188→
   189→        self.cleanup_if_needed(now);
   190→        decision
   191→    }
   192→
   193→    fn cleanup_if_needed(&self, now: Instant) {
   194→        let seen = self.requests_seen.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed) + 1;
   195→        if !seen.is_multiple_of(CLEANUP_EVERY_REQUESTS) {
   196→            return;
   197→        }
   198→        let stale_after = self.max_window().saturating_mul(3);
   199→        self.buckets
   200→            .retain(|_, state| now.duration_since(state.started_at) <= stale_after);
   201→    }
   202→
   203→    fn max_window(&self) -> Duration {
   204→        [
   205→            self.limits.login.window,
   206→            self.limits.auth_api.window,
   207→            self.limits.api.window,
   208→            self.limits.share.window,
   209→            self.limits.ws.window,
   210→        ]
   211→        .into_iter()
   212→        .max()
   213→        .unwrap_or(Duration::from_secs(60))
   214→    }
   215→}
   216→
   217→impl Default for RequestThrottle {
   218→    fn default() -> Self {
   219→        Self::new()
   220→    }
   221→}
   222→
   223→pub async fn throttle_gate(
   224→    State(state): State<AppState>,
   225→    ConnectInfo(addr): ConnectInfo<SocketAddr>,
   226→    request: axum::http::Request<axum::body::Body>,
   227→    next: Next,
   228→) -> Response {
   229→    let method = request.method().clone();
   230→    let path = request.uri().path().to_owned();
   231→    let Some(scope) = ThrottleScope::from_request(&method, &path) else {
   232→        return next.run(request).await;
   233→    };
   234→
   235→    if should_bypass_throttling(&state, request.headers(), addr).await {
   236→        return next.run(request).await;
   237→    }
   238→
   239→    let client_ip = resolve_client_ip(request.headers(), addr, state.gateway.behind_proxy);
   240→    match state.request_throttle.check(client_ip, scope) {
   241→        ThrottleDecision::Allowed => next.run(request).await,
   242→        ThrottleDecision::Denied { retry_after } => rate_limited_response(path, retry_after),
   243→    }
   244→}
   245→
   246→async fn should_bypass_throttling(state: &AppState, headers: &HeaderMap, addr: SocketAddr) -> bool {
   247→    let Some(store) = state.gateway.credential_store.as_ref() else {
   248→        // No credential store means auth is not enforced.
   249→        return true;
   250→    };
   251→
   252→    let is_local = crate::server::is_local_connection(headers, addr, state.gateway.behind_proxy);
   253→    matches!(
   254→        crate::auth_middleware::check_auth(store, headers, is_local).await,
   255→        crate::auth_middleware::AuthResult::Allowed(_)
   256→    )
   257→}
   258→
   259→fn rate_limited_response(path: String, retry_after: Duration) -> Response {
   260→    let retry_after_secs = retry_after.as_secs().max(1);
   261→    let mut response = if path.starts_with("/api/") {
   262→        (
   263→            StatusCode::TOO_MANY_REQUESTS,
   264→            Json(serde_json::json!({
   265→                "code": RATE_LIMITED,
   266→                "error": "too many requests",
   267→                "retry_after_seconds": retry_after_secs
   268→            })),
   269→        )
   270→            .into_response()
   271→    } else {
   272→        (
   273→            StatusCode::TOO_MANY_REQUESTS,
   274→            format!("too many requests, retry after {retry_after_secs}s"),
   275→        )
   276→            .into_response()
   277→    };
   278→
   279→    if let Ok(value) = retry_after_secs.to_string().parse() {
   280→        response
   281→            .headers_mut()
   282→            .insert(axum::http::header::RETRY_AFTER, value);
   283→    }
   284→    response
   285→}
   286→
   287→fn resolve_client_ip(headers: &HeaderMap, addr: SocketAddr, behind_proxy: bool) -> IpAddr {
   288→    if behind_proxy && let Some(ip) = extract_forwarded_ip(headers) {
   289→        return ip;
   290→    }
   291→    addr.ip()
   292→}
   293→
   294→fn extract_forwarded_ip(headers: &HeaderMap) -> Option<IpAddr> {
   295→    let xff = headers.get("x-forwarded-for").and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok());
   296→    if let Some(xff) = xff
   297→        && let Some(ip) = xff
   298→            .split(',')
   299→            .find_map(|candidate| parse_ip(candidate.trim()))
   300→    {
   301→        return Some(ip);
   302→    }
   303→
   304→    let xri = headers.get("x-real-ip").and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok());
   305→    if let Some(xri) = xri
   306→        && let Some(ip) = parse_ip(xri.trim())
   307→    {
   308→        return Some(ip);
   309→    }
   310→
   311→    let cf_ip = headers
   312→        .get("cf-connecting-ip")
   313→        .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok());
   314→    if let Some(cf_ip) = cf_ip
   315→        && let Some(ip) = parse_ip(cf_ip.trim())
   316→    {
   317→        return Some(ip);
   318→    }
   319→
```

  1221→/// Password login attempts are throttled to reduce brute-force attempts.
  1222→#[cfg(feature = "web-ui")]
  1223→#[tokio::test]
  1224→async fn login_endpoint_rate_limited_after_repeated_failures() {
  1225→    let (addr, store) = start_auth_server().await;
  1226→    store.set_initial_password("testpass123").await.unwrap();
  1227→
  1228→    let client = reqwest::Client::new();
  1229→
  1230→    for _ in 0..5 {
  1231→        let resp = client
  1232→            .post(format!("http://{addr}/api/auth/login"))
  1233→            .header("Content-Type", "application/json")
  1234→            .body(r#"{"password":"wrong-password"}"#)
  1235→            .send()
  1236→            .await
  1237→            .unwrap();
  1238→        assert_eq!(
  1239→            resp.status(),
  1240→            401,
  1241→            "login should fail before throttle engages"
  1242→        );
  1243→    }
  1244→
  1245→    let throttled = client
  1246→        .post(format!("http://{addr}/api/auth/login"))
  1247→        .header("Content-Type", "application/json")
  1248→        .body(r#"{"password":"wrong-password"}"#)
  1249→        .send()
  1250→        .await
  1251→        .unwrap();
  1252→
  1253→    assert_eq!(throttled.status(), 429);
  1254→
  1255→    let retry_after = throttled
  1256→        .headers()
  1257→        .get(reqwest::header::RETRY_AFTER)
  1258→        .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok())
  1259→        .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())
  1260→        .unwrap_or(0);
  1261→    assert!(
  1262→        retry_after >= 1,
  1263→        "expected Retry-After header on throttled login response"
  1264→    );
  1265→}
  1266→
  1267→/// Normal API endpoints are also throttled with a higher ceiling for regular use.
  1268→#[cfg(feature = "web-ui")]
  1269→#[tokio::test]
  1270→async fn api_endpoint_rate_limited_after_high_request_volume() {
  1271→    let (addr, store) = start_auth_server().await;
  1272→    store.set_initial_password("testpass123").await.unwrap();

  1274→    let client = reqwest::Client::new();
  1275→

  1278→            .get(format!("http://{addr}/api/bootstrap"))

  1284→            401,

  1286→        );
  1287→    }
  1288→
  1289→    let throttled = client

  1291→        .send()
  1292→        .await
  1293→        .unwrap();
  1294→
  1295→    assert_eq!(throttled.status(), 429);
  1296→}

    52→async fn start_auth_server_impl(
    53→    localhost_only: bool,
    54→    behind_proxy: bool,
    55→) -> (SocketAddr, Arc<CredentialStore>, Arc<GatewayState>) {
    56→    // Isolate each test process with its own config/data directory so
    57→    // concurrent nextest processes don't race on shared config files.
    58→    let tmp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    59→    moltis_config::set_config_dir(tmp.path().to_path_buf());
    60→    moltis_config::set_data_dir(tmp.path().to_path_buf());
    61→    // Leak the TempDir so it outlives the test (cleaned up on process exit).
    62→    std::mem::forget(tmp);
    63→
    64→    let pool = sqlx::SqlitePool::connect("sqlite::memory:").await.unwrap();
    65→    let auth_config = moltis_config::AuthConfig::default();
    66→    let cred_store = Arc::new(
    67→        CredentialStore::with_config(pool, &auth_config)
    68→            .await
    69→            .unwrap(),
    70→    );
    71→
    72→    let resolved_auth = auth::resolve_auth(None, None);
    73→    let services = GatewayServices::noop();
    74→    let state = GatewayState::with_options(
    75→        resolved_auth,
    76→        services,
    77→        moltis_config::MoltisConfig::default(),
    78→        None,
    79→        Some(Arc::clone(&cred_store)),
    80→        None, // pairing_store
    81→        localhost_only,
    82→        behind_proxy,
    83→        false,
    84→        None,
    85→        None,

Now I have a clear picture. Let me create the `LoginGuard` module. The approach:

1. **New module** `login_guard.rs` in `crates/httpd/src/` — tracks per-IP and per-account failed attempts
2. **IP ban**: After 10 failed logins from one IP → ban that IP for escalating duration (5min, 15min, 1hr, etc.)
3. **Account lockout**: After failures from 3+ distinct IPs → lock for 15 minutes
4. **Wire it** into `AuthState` and the `login_handler` + `passkey_auth_finish_handler`

{
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/httpd/src/login_guard.rs",
  "content": "//! Brute-force protection for authentication endpoints.\n//!\n//! Provides two complementary defences:\n//!\n//! 1. **IP ban** — after [`IP_BAN_THRESHOLD`] consecutive failures from a single\n//!    IP address the IP is banned with exponential back-off\n//!    (5 min → 15 min → 1 hour → 24 hours, capped).\n//!\n//! 2. **Account lockout** — after [`ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_IP_THRESHOLD`] *distinct* IPs\n//!    fail for the same account within [`ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_WINDOW`] the account is\n//!    locked for [`ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_DURATION`].\n//!\n//! Both layers are in-memory ([`DashMap`]) and cleaned up periodically.\n\nuse std::{\n    collections::HashSet,\n    net::IpAddr,\n    sync::{\n        Arc,\n        atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering},\n    },\n    time::{Duration, Instant},\n};\n\nuse dashmap::DashMap;\n\n// ── Tuning constants ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Consecutive failures from a single IP before it is banned.\nconst IP_BAN_THRESHOLD: u32 = 10;\n\n/// Base ban duration (first offence).\nconst IP_BAN_BASE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(5 * 60); // 5 minutes\n\n/// Maximum ban duration after repeated escalations.\nconst IP_BAN_CAP: Duration = Duration::from_secs(24 * 60 * 60); // 24 hours\n\n/// Number of distinct IPs that must fail for the same account\n/// within [`ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_WINDOW`] to trigger a lockout.\nconst ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_IP_THRESHOLD: usize = 3;\n\n/// Sliding window for counting distinct IPs per account.\nconst ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_WINDOW: Duration = Duration::from_secs(15 * 60); // 15 minutes\n\n/// How long a locked-out account stays locked.\nconst ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_DURATION: Duration = Duration::from_secs(15 * 60); // 15 minutes\n\n/// Run cleanup every N `record_failure` calls.\nconst CLEANUP_EVERY: u64 = 128;\n\n// ── Per-IP state ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\nstruct IpRecord {\n    /// Consecutive failures (reset on success).\n    consecutive_failures: u32,\n    /// Number of bans this IP has accumulated (drives escalation).\n    ban_count: u32,\n    /// If banned, when the ban expires.\n    banned_until: Option<Instant>,\n}\n\n// ── Per-account state ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\nstruct AccountRecord {\n    /// `(ip, first_failure_time)` pairs within the window.\n    recent_ips: Vec<(IpAddr, Instant)>,\n    /// If locked, when the lockout expires.\n    locked_until: Option<Instant>,\n}\n\n// ── Why a login attempt was rejected ─────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Reason a login attempt was blocked before credentials were checked.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum BlockReason {\n    /// The source IP is temporarily banned.\n    IpBanned {\n        retry_after: Duration,\n    },\n    /// The target account is temporarily locked.\n    AccountLocked {\n        retry_after: Duration,\n    },\n}\n\n// ── LoginGuard ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/// Thread-safe, in-memory brute-force guard.\n///\n/// Call [`check`] before verifying credentials and [`record_failure`] /\n/// [`record_success`] after the outcome is known.\n#[derive(Clone)]\npub struct LoginGuard {\n    ips: Arc<DashMap<IpAddr, IpRecord>>,\n    /// Keyed by \"account identifier\" — for password auth this is a fixed\n    /// sentinel (`\"__password__\"`) because moltis has a single admin account.\n    accounts: Arc<DashMap<String, AccountRecord>>,\n    calls: Arc<AtomicU64>,\n}\n\nimpl LoginGuard {\n    #[must_use]\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            ips: Arc::new(DashMap::new()),\n            accounts: Arc::new(DashMap::new()),\n            calls: Arc::new(AtomicU64::new(0)),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Check whether the request should be blocked *before* credential\n    /// verification.  Returns `None` if the attempt is allowed.\n    pub fn check(&self, ip: IpAddr, account: &str) -> Option<BlockReason> {\n        self.check_at(ip, account, Instant::now())\n    }\n\n    fn check_at(&self, ip: IpAddr, account: &str, now: Instant) -> Option<BlockReason> {\n        // Check IP ban first.\n        if let Some(rec) = self.ips.get(&ip) {\n            if let Some(until) = rec.banned_until {\n                if now < until {\n                    return Some(BlockReason::IpBanned {\n                        retry_after: until.duration_since(now),\n                    });\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Check account lockout.\n        if let Some(rec) = self.accounts.get(account) {\n            if let Some(until) = rec.locked_until {\n                if now < until {\n                    return Some(BlockReason::AccountLocked {\n                        retry_after: until.duration_since(now),\n                    });\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        None\n    }\n\n    /// Record a failed authentication attempt.\n    pub fn record_failure(&self, ip: IpAddr, account: &str) {\n        self.record_failure_at(ip, account, Instant::now());\n    }\n\n    fn record_failure_at(&self, ip: IpAddr, account: &str, now: Instant) {\n        // ── Update per-IP record ─────────────────────────────────────────\n        let mut ip_entry = self.ips.entry(ip).or_insert_with(|| IpRecord {\n            consecutive_failures: 0,\n            ban_count: 0,\n            banned_until: None,\n        });\n\n        ip_entry.consecutive_failures += 1;\n\n        if ip_entry.consecutive_failures >= IP_BAN_THRESHOLD {\n            ip_entry.ban_count += 1;\n            let multiplier = 1u32 << ip_entry.ban_count.saturating_sub(1).min(10);\n            let ban_duration = (IP_BAN_BASE * multiplier).min(IP_BAN_CAP);\n            ip_entry.banned_until = Some(now + ban_duration);\n            // Reset counter so the next window starts fresh after the ban.\n            ip_entry.consecutive_failures = 0;\n        }\n        drop(ip_entry);\n\n        // ── Update per-account record ────────────────────────────────────\n        let mut acct_entry = self\n            .accounts\n            .entry(account.to_owned())\n            .or_insert_with(|| AccountRecord {\n                recent_ips: Vec::new(),\n                locked_until: None,\n            });\n\n        // Prune IPs outside the window.\n        acct_entry\n            .recent_ips\n            .retain(|&(_, t)| now.duration_since(t) < ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_WINDOW);\n\n        // Add this IP if not already tracked in the current window.\n        if !acct_entry.recent_ips.iter().any(|&(recorded_ip, _)| recorded_ip == ip) {\n            acct_entry.recent_ips.push((ip, now));\n        }\n\n        let distinct_ips: HashSet<IpAddr> = acct_entry.recent_ips.iter().map(|&(ip, _)| ip).collect();\n        if distinct_ips.len() >= ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_IP_THRESHOLD {\n            acct_entry.locked_until = Some(now + ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_DURATION);\n            acct_entry.recent_ips.clear();\n        }\n        drop(acct_entry);\n\n        // Periodic cleanup.\n        let calls = self.calls.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed) + 1;\n        if calls.is_multiple_of(CLEANUP_EVERY) {\n            self.cleanup(now);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Record a successful authentication — clears IP failure counter.\n    pub fn record_success(&self, ip: IpAddr) {\n        if let Some(mut rec) = self.ips.get_mut(&ip) {\n            rec.consecutive_failures = 0;\n            // Clear the ban if it's expired (or let it ride if still active —\n            // a successful login means the request wasn't blocked, so the ban\n            // must have already expired).\n            rec.banned_until = None;\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn cleanup(&self, now: Instant) {\n        // Remove expired IP bans with no recent failures.\n        self.ips.retain(|_, rec| {\n            if rec.consecutive_failures > 0 {\n                return true;\n            }\n            match rec.banned_until {\n                Some(until) => now < until,\n                None => false,\n            }\n        });\n\n        // Remove expired account lockouts with no recent IPs.\n        self.accounts.retain(|_, rec| {\n            rec.recent_ips\n                .retain(|&(_, t)| now.duration_since(t) < ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_WINDOW);\n            if !rec.recent_ips.is_empty() {\n                return true;\n            }\n            match rec.locked_until {\n                Some(until) => now < until,\n                None => false,\n            }\n        });\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Default for LoginGuard {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self::new()\n    }\n}\n\n// ── Tests ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use std::net::Ipv4Addr;\n\n    use super::*;\n\n    fn ip(last: u8) -> IpAddr {\n        IpAddr::V4(Ipv4Addr::new(10, 0, 0, last))\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn allows_initial_attempt() {\n        let guard = LoginGuard::new();\n        assert!(guard.check(ip(1), \"admin\").is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn bans_ip_after_threshold() {\n        let guard = LoginGuard::new();\n        let now = Instant::now();\n\n        // 10 failures should trigger ban.\n        for i in 0..IP_BAN_THRESHOLD {\n            assert!(\n                guard.check_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now).is_none(),\n                \"attempt {i} should be allowed\"\n            );\n            guard.record_failure_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n        }\n\n        // Next attempt is blocked.\n        let block = guard.check_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n        assert!(\n            matches!(block, Some(BlockReason::IpBanned { .. })),\n            \"expected IP ban, got {block:?}\"\n        );\n\n        // A different IP is not affected.\n        assert!(guard.check_at(ip(2), \"admin\", now).is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn ip_ban_expires() {\n        let guard = LoginGuard::new();\n        let now = Instant::now();\n\n        for _ in 0..IP_BAN_THRESHOLD {\n            guard.record_failure_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n        }\n        assert!(guard.check_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now).is_some());\n\n        // After ban duration + 1 second, should be allowed again.\n        let after = now + IP_BAN_BASE + Duration::from_secs(1);\n        assert!(guard.check_at(ip(1), \"admin\", after).is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn ip_ban_escalates() {\n        let guard = LoginGuard::new();\n        let mut now = Instant::now();\n\n        // First ban: 5 minutes.\n        for _ in 0..IP_BAN_THRESHOLD {\n            guard.record_failure_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n        }\n        if let Some(BlockReason::IpBanned { retry_after }) = guard.check_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now) {\n            assert!(\n                retry_after <= IP_BAN_BASE,\n                \"first ban should be ~5min, got {retry_after:?}\"\n            );\n        } else {\n            panic!(\"expected ban\");\n        }\n\n        // Advance past first ban.\n        now += IP_BAN_BASE + Duration::from_secs(1);\n\n        // Second ban: 10 minutes.\n        for _ in 0..IP_BAN_THRESHOLD {\n            guard.record_failure_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n        }\n        if let Some(BlockReason::IpBanned { retry_after }) = guard.check_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now) {\n            assert!(\n                retry_after > IP_BAN_BASE,\n                \"second ban should be escalated, got {retry_after:?}\"\n            );\n        } else {\n            panic!(\"expected escalated ban\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn success_clears_ip_state() {\n        let guard = LoginGuard::new();\n        let now = Instant::now();\n\n        for _ in 0..(IP_BAN_THRESHOLD - 1) {\n            guard.record_failure_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n        }\n\n        // One more failure would ban — but success resets.\n        guard.record_success(ip(1));\n        guard.record_failure_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n        assert!(\n            guard.check_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now).is_none(),\n            \"counter should have reset on success\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn account_locks_after_distinct_ips() {\n        let guard = LoginGuard::new();\n        let now = Instant::now();\n\n        // Failures from 3 distinct IPs targeting the same account.\n        guard.record_failure_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n        guard.record_failure_at(ip(2), \"admin\", now);\n        assert!(\n            guard.check_at(ip(4), \"admin\", now).is_none(),\n            \"should not be locked after 2 IPs\"\n        );\n\n        guard.record_failure_at(ip(3), \"admin\", now);\n\n        // Account is now locked — even a new IP is blocked.\n        let block = guard.check_at(ip(4), \"admin\", now);\n        assert!(\n            matches!(block, Some(BlockReason::AccountLocked { .. })),\n            \"expected account lockout, got {block:?}\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn account_lockout_expires() {\n        let guard = LoginGuard::new();\n        let now = Instant::now();\n\n        guard.record_failure_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n        guard.record_failure_at(ip(2), \"admin\", now);\n        guard.record_failure_at(ip(3), \"admin\", now);\n\n        assert!(guard.check_at(ip(4), \"admin\", now).is_some());\n\n        let after = now + ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_DURATION + Duration::from_secs(1);\n        assert!(guard.check_at(ip(4), \"admin\", after).is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn account_lockout_requires_ips_within_window() {\n        let guard = LoginGuard::new();\n        let now = Instant::now();\n\n        // IP 1 fails at t=0.\n        guard.record_failure_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n\n        // IP 2 fails after the window expires — IP 1 should be pruned.\n        let later = now + ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_WINDOW + Duration::from_secs(1);\n        guard.record_failure_at(ip(2), \"admin\", later);\n        guard.record_failure_at(ip(3), \"admin\", later);\n\n        // Only 2 IPs in window (ip(1) expired), so no lockout.\n        assert!(guard.check_at(ip(4), \"admin\", later).is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn ip_ban_checked_before_account_lockout() {\n        let guard = LoginGuard::new();\n        let now = Instant::now();\n\n        // Ban IP first.\n        for _ in 0..IP_BAN_THRESHOLD {\n            guard.record_failure_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n        }\n\n        let block = guard.check_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n        assert!(\n            matches!(block, Some(BlockReason::IpBanned { .. })),\n            \"IP ban should take precedence\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn cleanup_removes_stale_entries() {\n        let guard = LoginGuard::new();\n        let now = Instant::now();\n\n        guard.record_failure_at(ip(1), \"admin\", now);\n\n        // Well past all windows and bans.\n        let far_future = now + Duration::from_secs(100_000);\n        guard.cleanup(far_future);\n\n        assert!(guard.ips.is_empty());\n        assert!(guard.accounts.is_empty());\n    }\n}\n"
}

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01SjXWkU8C81GE42ZyoyHggc
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/httpd/src/auth_routes.rs",
  "old_string": "// ── Status ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────",
  "new_string": "// ── Brute-force block response ────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nfn blocked_response(reason: crate::login_guard::BlockReason) -> axum::response::Response {\n    use crate::login_guard::BlockReason;\n    let (message, retry_after) = match reason {\n        BlockReason::IpBanned { retry_after } => {\n            (\"too many failed attempts from this address — try again later\", retry_after)\n        },\n        BlockReason::AccountLocked { retry_after } => {\n            (\"account temporarily locked due to suspicious activity — try again later\", retry_after)\n        },\n    };\n    let retry_secs = retry_after.as_secs().max(1);\n    let mut resp = (\n        StatusCode::TOO_MANY_REQUESTS,\n        Json(serde_json::json!({\n            \"code\": \"LOGIN_BLOCKED\",\n            \"error\": message,\n            \"retry_after_seconds\": retry_secs,\n        })),\n    )\n        .into_response();\n    if let Ok(val) = retry_secs.to_string().parse() {\n        resp.headers_mut()\n            .insert(axum::http::header::RETRY_AFTER, val);\n    }\n    resp\n}\n\n// ── Status ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/httpd/src/auth_routes.rs",
  "old_string": "async fn passkey_auth_finish_handler(\n    State(state): State<AuthState>,\n    Host(host): Host,\n    headers: axum::http::HeaderMap,\n    Json(body): Json<PasskeyAuthFinishRequest>,\n) -> impl IntoResponse {\n    let Some(ref registry) = state.webauthn_registry else {\n        return (StatusCode::NOT_IMPLEMENTED, \"passkeys not configured\").into_response();\n    };\n    let Some(wa) = host_to_webauthn(&host, registry).await else {\n        return (\n            StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST,\n            \"no passkey config for this hostname\",\n        )\n            .into_response();\n    };\n\n    match wa.finish_authentication(&body.challenge_id, &body.credential) {\n        Ok(_result) => match state.credential_store.create_session().await {\n            Ok(token) => {\n                let bp = state.gateway_state.behind_proxy;\n                session_response(token, &headers, bp, state.gateway_state.tls_active || bp)\n            },\n            Err(e) => (StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, e.to_string()).into_response(),\n        },\n        Err(e) => (StatusCode::UNAUTHORIZED, e.to_string()).into_response(),\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "async fn passkey_auth_finish_handler(\n    State(state): State<AuthState>,\n    ConnectInfo(addr): ConnectInfo<SocketAddr>,\n    Host(host): Host,\n    headers: axum::http::HeaderMap,\n    Json(body): Json<PasskeyAuthFinishRequest>,\n) -> impl IntoResponse {\n    let client_ip = crate::request_throttle::resolve_client_ip(\n        &headers,\n        addr,\n        state.gateway_state.behind_proxy,\n    );\n    const PASSKEY_ACCOUNT: &str = \"__passkey__\";\n\n    if let Some(block) = state.login_guard.check(client_ip, PASSKEY_ACCOUNT) {\n        return blocked_response(block);\n    }\n\n    let Some(ref registry) = state.webauthn_registry else {\n        return (StatusCode::NOT_IMPLEMENTED, \"passkeys not configured\").into_response();\n    };\n    let Some(wa) = host_to_webauthn(&host, registry).await else {\n        return (\n            StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST,\n            \"no passkey config for this hostname\",\n        )\n            .into_response();\n    };\n\n    match wa.finish_authentication(&body.challenge_id, &body.credential) {\n        Ok(_result) => {\n            state.login_guard.record_success(client_ip);\n            match state.credential_store.create_session().await {\n                Ok(token) => {\n                    let bp = state.gateway_state.behind_proxy;\n                    session_response(token, &headers, bp, state.gateway_state.tls_active || bp)\n                },\n                Err(e) => (StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, e.to_string()).into_response(),\n            }\n        },\n        Err(e) => {\n            state.login_guard.record_failure(client_ip, PASSKEY_ACCOUNT);\n            (StatusCode::UNAUTHORIZED, e.to_string()).into_response()\n        },\n    }\n}"
}

  "new_string": "    fn check_at(&self, ip: IpAddr, account: &str, now: Instant) -> Option<BlockReason> {\n        // Check IP ban first.\n        if let Some(rec) = self.ips.get(&ip)\n            && let Some(until) = rec.banned_until\n            && now < until\n        {\n            return Some(BlockReason::IpBanned {\n                retry_after: until.duration_since(now),\n            });\n        }\n\n        // Check account lockout.\n        if let Some(rec) = self.accounts.get(account)\n            && let Some(until) = rec.locked_until\n            && now < until\n        {\n            return Some(BlockReason::AccountLocked {\n                retry_after: until.duration_since(now),\n            });\n        }\n\n        None\n    }"
}

The cleanup logic retains IPs with `consecutive_failures > 0`. That's correct behavior — we want to keep tracking active attackers. Let me fix the cleanup to also expire entries whose last activity was too old. Actually, looking at the cleanup function:

   214→        // Remove expired IP bans with no recent failures.
   215→        self.ips.retain(|_, rec| {
   216→            if rec.consecutive_failures > 0 {
   217→                return true;
   218→            }
   219→            match rec.banned_until {

   228→                .retain(|&(_, t)| now.duration_since(t) < ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_WINDOW);

> AGENT

The problem is that an IP with `consecutive_failures > 0` but no ban is kept forever. That's a memory leak for one-off failed attempts. I should add a staleness check — if the record hasn't been updated in a long time, it can be cleaned up. But I don't track `last_activity` currently. The simplest fix: a failure counter without a ban and no recent activity should also be cleaned up. Let me add a `last_failure_at` field:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01QBkn4tfBXndPzQcDRKXm2U
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/quiver-fact/crates/httpd/src/login_guard.rs",
  "old_string": "#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\nstruct IpRecord {\n    /// Consecutive failures (reset on success).\n    consecutive_failures: u32,\n    /// Number of bans this IP has accumulated (drives escalation).\n    ban_count: u32,\n    /// If banned, when the ban expires.\n    banned_until: Option<Instant>,\n}",
  "new_string": "#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\nstruct IpRecord {\n    /// Consecutive failures (reset on success).\n    consecutive_failures: u32,\n    /// Number of bans this IP has accumulated (drives escalation).\n    ban_count: u32,\n    /// If banned, when the ban expires.\n    banned_until: Option<Instant>,\n    /// When the last failure was recorded (for staleness cleanup).\n    last_failure_at: Instant,\n}"
}
```

Keep if:\n        // - actively banned, OR\n        // - has recent failures (within the lockout window).\n        self.ips.retain(|_, rec| {\n            if let Some(until) = rec.banned_until {\n                if now < until {\n                    return true;\n                }\n            }\n            // Keep if there were recent failures (could still contribute\n            // to a ban if more arrive soon).\n            rec.consecutive_failures > 0\n                && now.duration_since(rec.last_failure_at) < ACCOUNT_LOCKOUT_WINDOW\n        });"

> AGENT

Pushed. The PR at https://github.com/moltis-org/moltis/pull/682 now includes both commits. Here's what the `LoginGuard` does:

**IP ban** (single attacker):
- 10 consecutive failures → banned for 5 minutes
- Repeat offence → 10 min, 20 min, 40 min... up to 24-hour cap
- Successful login resets the counter and clears the ban

**Account lockout** (distributed attack):
- 3 distinct IPs fail the same account within 15 minutes → account locked for 15 minutes
- Protects against credential stuffing from botnets

Both password (`/api/auth/login`) and passkey (`/api/auth/passkey/auth/finish`) endpoints are guarded. Blocked requests get 429 + `Retry-After` + descriptive JSON error.
